<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every domain in the Riemann sphere is conformally equivalent to a circle domain, resolving Koebe's circle-domain conjecture positively.

- **claim-main-uniformization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Every nonempty connected open set $G\subset\widehat{\mathbb C}$ is conformally equivalent to a circle domain.
  - Explanation: Every domain in the Riemann sphere admits a conformal map onto a circle domain, so Koebe's circle-domain conjecture holds with no restriction on the number or regularity of complementary components.
- **claim-quotient-sphere** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-quotient)): Collapse each component of $\widehat{\mathbb C}\setminus G$ to a point, and leave the points of $G$ as singleton decomposition elements. The resulting quotient map $$\pi:\widehat{\mathbb C}\longrightarrow X$$ is a closed monotone map onto a topological sphere. Its restriction to $G$ is a homeomorphism onto an open subset of $X$. The set $$B=X\setminus\pi(G)$$ is compact and totally disconnected. Moreover, the inverse image under $\pi$ of every continuum in $X$ is connected.
  - Explanation: Collapsing each complementary component of the domain to a point gives a topological sphere. The collapsed boundary set is compact and totally disconnected, and preimages of continua are connected.
- **claim-small-disks-contours** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dust-disks)): The following assertions hold.* 1. *If $F\subset X$ is closed, every nondegenerate continuum contained in $F\cup B$ is contained in $F$.* 2. *Suppose that $F\subset X$ is closed and contains $B$, and that $\{x\}$ is a component of $F$, with $x\ne\infty$. Every neighborhood of $x$ contains the closure of a dust disk containing $x$ whose boundary misses $F$.* 3. *Let $A$ be a compact relatively clopen subset of $B$, and let $W\subset X\setminus\{\infty\}$ be open with $A\subset W$. For every $\varepsilon>0$, there are finitely many dust disks with pairwise disjoint closures, all contained with their closures in $W$, of $d_X$-diameter less than $\varepsilon$, whose union contains $A$. The disks can be required to avoid $B\setminus A$ and to meet $A$.* 4. *Suppose that finitely many dust disks have closures in a Jordan domain $V\subset X\setminus\{\infty\}$, and their boundaries avoid a closed set $F\supset B$. Their closures can be covered by finitely many Jordan disks with pairwise disjoint closures in $V$ and smooth boundaries in $G\setminus F$.
  - Explanation: Boundary points admit small disks with smooth contours inside the domain avoiding prescribed closed sets. This allows a compact exhaustion of the domain by smooth bordered cores.
- **claim-generic-level-continua** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-level-continua)): For each finite list $i_1,\ldots,i_d$, almost every tuple $(t_1,\ldots,t_d)$ gives a compact level union $$H=\bigcup_{k=1}^d H_{i_k}(t_k)$$ with the following properties.* 1. *$\mathcal H^1_{\mathrm{sph}}(H\cap G)<\infty$.* 2. *For each $\varepsilon>0$ there is a finite bound on the cardinality of any family of pairwise disjoint subcontinua of $H$ having $d_X$-diameter at least $\varepsilon$.* 3. *$H$ has at most countably many nondegenerate components, and every subcontinuum of $H$ is locally connected. Any two distinct points in a connected relatively open subset of such a subcontinuum can be joined by a simple arc within that subset.* *In addition, if $q\ge0$ is Borel on $G$ and $m\ge0$ is in $\ell^2(B)$, then for each label $i$, $$\label{eq:weighted-level-bound} \begin{split} &\int_{\mathbb T}\left( \int_{H_i(t)\cap G}q\,\mathrm d\mathcal H^1_{\mathrm{sph}} +\sum_{s\in H_i(t)\cap B}m(s)\right)\mathrm dt\\ &\hspace{15mm}\le \int_{G\cap M_i}q\|\mathrm du_i\|\,\mathrm da +\sum_{s\in B}m(s)a_i(s). \end{split}$$ The right side is finite when $q\in L^2(G,\mathrm da)$.
  - Explanation: For almost every choice of levels, a finite union of level sets has finite length in the domain, only countably many nondegenerate components, and locally connected subcontinua. A weighted coarea bound controls integrated level contributions.
- **claim-trace-null-image** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-null)): Let $L$ be a locally connected metric continuum, let $V\subset L$ be relatively open, and let $\nu$ be a finite Borel measure on $L$. Suppose $F\subset V$ has $\nu$-outer measure zero and a function $v:F\to\mathbb R$ satisfies $$\|v(x)-v(y)\|\le A\nu(\alpha)$$ for some finite $A\ge0$, every $x,y\in F$, and every simple arc $\alpha\subset V$ joining $x$ and $y$. Then $v(F)$ has Lebesgue outer measure zero.* *The same conclusion holds locally when $F$ is covered by countably many such relatively open sets and $v$ has a real branch on each. In particular, the corresponding image in $\mathbb T$ has Haar measure zero for a circle-valued trace with these local branches.
  - Explanation: If a function varies along every simple arc by at most a constant times a finite measure of the arc, and is defined on a null set, then its image has Lebesgue outer measure zero. It is used to verify compatibility.
- **claim-finite-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-transfer)): Let $u_1,\ldots,u_m$ be a finite ordered list satisfying Definition <a href="#def:coordinates" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:coordinates">4</a> and compatibility (P), with a common countable flag set $\Sigma$. The same assertion holds for a finite ordered sublist of a countable compatible list. Prescribe a finite set $F\subset B$ containing all singular points of the list, a compact core $K_0\subset G$, a dust-disk cover from the fixed exhaustion, and $\varepsilon>0$.* *There exist a smooth compact core $K\subset G$, finitely many terminal disks $T_s$ with pairwise disjoint closures, indexed by a finite set $S\subset B$ containing $F$, and a smooth metric $g$ on the physical sphere, with the following properties. Every terminal disk lies in a member of the prescribed cover, $s\in T_s$, and $K$ contains $K_0$ and all mask boundaries in its interior. The metric $g$ agrees with the spherical metric on a neighborhood of $K$. Write $$Q=\widehat{\mathbb C}\setminus\bigcup_{s\in S}T_s, \qquad Q^\circ=\widehat{\mathbb C}\setminus\bigcup_{s\in S}\overline{T_s}.$$ Then $K\subset Q^\circ$, and $Q$, equipped with $g$, is a smooth bordered surface of genus zero. Each $u_i$ has a transferred coordinate $u_i^*$ on $Q\cap M_i$, smooth up to its boundary in local real branches, with the following properties.* 1. *$u_i^*=u_i$ on a neighborhood of $K\cap M_i$;* 2. *The total energy outside $K$ satisfies $$\label{eq:transfer-tail} \sum_{i=1}^m \\|\mathrm du_i^*\\|_{2,(Q\setminus K)\cap M_i,g}^2<\varepsilon^2;$$* 3. *For each nonsingular terminal index $s\in S\cap M_i$, a single-valued lift on a collar of $\partial T_s$ has boundary oscillation $A_i(T_s)$ satisfying $$\label{eq:transfer-oscillation} \left(\sum_{\substack{s\in S\cap M_i\\s\ne p_i}} [A_i(T_s)-a_i(s)]_+^2\right)^{1/2}<\varepsilon.$$ Here $[x]_+=\max\{x,0\}$. For a real coordinate the restriction $s\ne p_i$ is omitted.* 4. *A periodic coordinate still adds $1$ under its positive deck translation. Its differential has period $1$ around the terminal disk indexed by its singular point, and period $0$ around every other terminal disk in its mask. Here a positive circuit around a terminal disk is counterclockwise around that disk.* *The core $K_0$ may contain any prescribed compact subsets of $G$. In particular the conformal structure on $\{\|z\|\ge R\}$ can always be retained. Terminal disks can be required to lie in arbitrarily small prescribed dust neighborhoods of their indices.
  - Explanation: A finite compatible list of coordinates transfers to a smooth bordered genus-zero surface. The coordinates are unchanged on a prescribed core, with arbitrarily small added energy and excess boundary oscillation, and periods are preserved exactly.
- **claim-normalized-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-normalized-limits)): There is a constant $L$, depending only on $R$, with the following properties. For every completion just described, all complementary disks of its normalized circle domain and all images of physical points in its domain with $\|z\|\le4R$ lie in $\{\|w\|<L\}$. One may take $L=5R$. After passage to a subsequence, $f_n$ converges locally uniformly on $G$ to a normalized univalent map $f$. Simultaneously, for every fixed mark $b$, the disks $D_{n,b}$ converge in the Hausdorff metric to a closed round disk $D_b$, possibly a singleton.* *The same bound $L$ holds for the bounded complementary components and the images of $G\cap\{\|z\|\le4R\}$ for every normalized univalent map on $G$.
  - Explanation: Normalized circle-domain maps from the completions have complementary disks and images in a fixed disk. A subsequence converges locally uniformly to a normalized univalent map, and marked disks converge in the Hausdorff metric.
- **claim-end-components** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-end-components)): Let $f$ be a normalized univalent map on $G$. For $b\in B$ set $$\label{eq:transfer-end-component} K_b(f)=\bigcap_{j\ge1}\mathcal Q_f(C_j(b)).$$ These sets are exactly the complementary components of $f(G)$, with one component for each $b\in B$. They are nonempty continua, and $$K_b(f)\subset\operatorname{int}\mathcal Q_f(C_j(b)) \quad\text{for every }j.$$ For a sequence and marked disk limits as in Lemma <a href="#lem:normalized-limits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:normalized-limits">9</a>, $$\label{eq:transfer-mark-trapping} D_b\subset K_b(f).$$* *In a single finite completion, each terminal boundary corresponds to exactly one complementary round disk. Points of the circle domain approaching that disk have preimages approaching the corresponding terminal boundary as a set. Thus the cluster oscillation of a transferred coordinate at a nonsingular disk is at most its boundary oscillation $A_i(T_s)$, in a single-valued lift on a collar.
  - Explanation: Complementary components of the image of a normalized univalent map correspond to boundary points via nested images of source collars. Limiting marked disks lie inside the corresponding component.
- **claim-compatible-barrier** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-barrier)): Use any fixed path system of Definition <a href="#def:path-system" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:path-system">12</a> and any endpoint data of Definition <a href="#def:endpoint" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:endpoint">11</a>. Suppose $g\in L^2(G)$ is nonnegative Borel and bounded below by a positive constant, and $m\in\ell^2(B)$ is nonnegative. Assume that every allowed path from $E$ to $b$ which first reaches $b$ at time $1$ and obeys the gates, if present, satisfies $$\mathcal L^-_{g,m}(\gamma)\ge1.$$ Then for every $\varepsilon>0$ there is a barrier $u$, and an allowed penalty region $O$ when gates are present, such that $$\label{eq:barrier-bound} \\|\mathrm du\\|_{2,G\setminus O}+\\|a_u\\|_{\ell^2(B)} \le C_{\mathrm{bar}} \bigl(\\|g\\|_2+\\|m\\|_{\ell^2(B)}\bigr)+\varepsilon, \qquad C_{\mathrm{bar}}=8.$$ One may take $0\le u\le1$, $a_u=4m$, and enlarge the flags only by $\{m>0\}$. The numerical constant is independent of the prior list, earlier laws, atomless measure, null tuple exclusions, endpoint data, and positive lower bound on $g$.
  - Explanation: If every allowed path to a boundary point has cost at least one, a compatible smooth barrier exists with energy and budget bounded by a universal constant times the metric norms plus an arbitrary error.
- **claim-path-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-path-stability)): Allowed paths are stable under reversal, affine reparametrization, and restriction to a closed parameter subinterval. More generally, suppose a continuous path is made from countably many restrictions of allowed paths, possibly reversed, on disjoint open parameter intervals. Assume that the complement of those intervals contains only countably many times and that the resulting path has finite domain-part length. Then the path can be tagged so as to be allowed.
  - Explanation: Allowed tagged paths are stable under reversal, reparametrization and restriction, and under countable concatenations with countably many extra junction times.
- **claim-path-coding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-path-coding)): For fixed path-system data, the allowed tagged codes form a standard Borel space. On this space the domain-part length and the arclength occupation measure are Borel. For every fixed nonnegative Borel $g\in L^2(G)$ and fixed nonnegative $m\in\ell^2(B)$, all three costs $\mathcal L_{g,m}$, $\mathcal L^-_{g,m}$, and $\mathcal L^\circ_{g,m}$ are Borel, with values in $[0,\infty]$. Endpoint restrictions, first arrival at a prescribed dust point at time $1$, and hit or avoidance conditions for closed or relatively open subsets of prescribed compact collar curves are Borel.
  - Explanation: Allowed tagged codes form a standard Borel space on which length, occupation measure and all path costs are Borel, so optimization over paths and laws on codes make sense.
- **claim-collar-concatenation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-collar-splicing)): Fix a path system and a metric $(g,m)$ with $g\ge c_0>0$ on $G$. Let allowed paths $\gamma_n$, whose endpoints are in $G$, have costs converging to a finite number $A$.* 1. *If their initial and final endpoints converge to distinct points $p,q\in B$, then for every $\eta>0$ there is an allowed path $\gamma$ from $p$ to $q$ satisfying $$\mathcal L^\circ_{g,m}(\gamma)\le A+\eta.$$* 2. *If their initial endpoints belong to a set $F\subset G$ and their final endpoints converge to $p\in B\setminus\overline F$, where closure is in $X$, there is an allowed path from a point of $F$ to $p$ with $\mathcal L^-_{g,m}\le A+\eta$.* *The paths produced consist of countably many restricted original pieces and joining arcs on smooth collar curves in $G$. Every dust point avoided by the original pieces remains avoided away from the newly adjoined endpoint times. Any prescribed hit restrictions are also preserved when the joining collars are disjoint from their hit curves.
  - Explanation: Paths whose endpoints approach dust points can be spliced along collar curves into an allowed path reaching those points, at cost within an arbitrary tolerance of the limit.
- **claim-jump-removal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-jump-removal)): Fix a metric $(g,m)$ with $g\ge c_0>0$, a compact anchor $E\subset G$, and a compact collar curve $C\subset G$. Suppose that, with finitely many fixed jump levels, every chain from $E$ to $C$ has cost at least $\beta>0$. Fix $\rho,\tau>0$ and $0<\Delta<\beta$. For all sufficiently small meshes, adding any new jump level whose disk $V$ has toll $$\label{eq:neighborhood-jump-cost} c(V)=\max\left\{\rho, \sup_{\substack{s\in B\\\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_X(s,V)<\tau}}m(s)\right\}$$ preserves the lower bound $\beta-\Delta$ for chains from $E$ to $C$. Here each new disk meets $B$ and its closure misses $E$ and $C$.
  - Explanation: For sufficiently fine meshes, adding a new level of small jumps with the stated tolls lowers the minimal chain cost from the anchor to the collar by at most a prescribed amount.
- **claim-positive-law-alternative** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-positive-law)): Suppose that, for some $\beta>0$, there is no new compatible barrier coordinate with $$\\|\mathrm du\\|_{2,G\setminus O}+\\|a_u\\|_{\ell^2(B)}<\beta.$$ Nonexistence here allows an arbitrary countable enlargement of the flags and, in the gated problem, any penalty region permitted in Definition <a href="#def:endpoint" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:endpoint">11</a>. Then there is a probability law $\mu$ on base codes with the following properties:* 1. *$\bar\ell_\mu=g_\mu\,\mathrm da$, where $g_\mu\in L^2(G,\mathrm da)$;* 2. *$n_\mu\|_{D_*}\in\ell^2(D_*)$ and $m_\mu\in\ell^2(B)$;* 3. *for every slot and every tag type, its tuple distribution, regarded as a subprobability measure, is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure on the corresponding tuple torus.* *The three norms in <span class="upright">(i)–(ii)</span> are bounded by a constant depending only on $\beta$ and the numerical constant in Theorem <a href="#thm:barrier" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:barrier">13</a>. In particular, the bound does not depend on any auxiliary countable subset of $B$ or any auxiliary finite atomless measure on $B$.
  - Explanation: If no small compatible barrier exists, there is a probability law on base codes with square-integrable occupation density, square-summable visit counts on the prior data, square-summable hit probabilities on all dust, and absolutely continuous tag distributions.
- **claim-modulus-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-modulus-duality)): Let $Y$ be Polish, let $\sigma$ be a finite Borel measure on $Y$, let $A$ be standard Borel, and let $a\mapsto\nu_a\in\mathcal M_+(Y)$ be Borel. If $\mathop{\mathrm{Mod}}_\sigma(A)>c>0$, then, for every $C>c^{-1/2}$, there is a Borel probability measure $P$ on $A$ whose barycenter satisfies $$\int_A\nu_a\,\mathrm dP(a)=q\,\sigma, \qquad q\ge0, \qquad \\|q\\|_{L^2(\sigma)}\le C.$$ Moreover, after giving $A$ a Polish topology with its original Borel sets and with $a\mapsto\nu_a$ continuous, the law may be supported on a compact subset of $A$.
  - Explanation: A positive lower bound on the modulus of a Borel family of measures yields a probability measure whose barycenter has a bounded density in the reference space, with compact support after a refinement of the topology.
- **claim-countable-law-extraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-countable-law)): Under the hypothesis of Theorem <a href="#thm:positive-law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:positive-law">19</a>, there is a constant $C<\infty$, depending only on $\beta$ and the constant in Theorem <a href="#thm:barrier" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:barrier">13</a>, with the following property. For every countable $D\supset D_*\cup\{b\}$ and every finite atomless Borel measure $\lambda$ on $B$, there is a law $\mu$ on base codes such that $$\begin{aligned} \bar\ell_\mu&=g_\mu\,\mathrm da, &\\|g_\mu\\|_2&\le C,\label{eq:countable-law-occupation}\\ \\|n_\mu\|_D\\|_{\ell^2(D)}&\le C+1, &\int_Bm_\mu\,\mathrm d\lambda&=0,\label{eq:countable-law-dust}\\ \mathop{\mathrm{\mathbb E}}_\mu\psi_\Gamma&=q_\mu\,\tau, &\\|q_\mu\\|_{L^2(\tau)}&\le C.\label{eq:countable-law-tuples} \end{aligned}$$ In addition, $\lambda(\gamma(Z_\Gamma))=0$ for almost every code.
  - Explanation: Under the failure of small barriers, duality gives for any prescribed countable set and atomless measure a path law with uniform occupation, visit-count and tuple-density bounds and no mass on the atomless measure.
- **claim-law-fusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-law-fusion)): Let $S$ be standard Borel, let $B$ be compact metric, and let $\mathcal M$ be a Borel family of probability measures on $S$, closed under Borel probability mixtures. Suppose a Borel relation $\mathcal H\subset S\times B$ defines $$m_z(s)=z\{x\in S:(x,s)\in\mathcal H\}\in[0,1].$$ Assume that $\mathcal M$ has a Polish topology generating its Borel sets for which $(z,s)\mapsto m_z(s)$ is jointly upper semicontinuous. Finally, suppose that for every countable $D\subset B$ and every finite atomless Borel measure $\lambda$ on $B$, some $z\in\mathcal M$ satisfies $$\label{eq:fusion-countable-tests} \\|m_z\|_D\\|_{\ell^2(D)}\le C_0, \qquad \int_Bm_z\,\mathrm d\lambda=0.$$ Then, for every $\varepsilon>0$, there exists $\bar z\in\mathcal M$ with $\\|m_{\bar z}\\|_{\ell^2(B)}\le C_0+\varepsilon$.
  - Explanation: A mixture-closed family of laws meeting countable and atomless tests with a common bound contains a single law whose hit probabilities obey a nearly equal square-summable bound on the whole boundary set.
- **claim-point-avoidance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-point-avoidance)): Let $\mu$ be a law of paths ending in $B$, with expected domain arclength occupation $g_\mu\,\mathrm da$ and $g_\mu\in L^2(G)$. Every fixed point of $G$ is visited with probability zero. In particular, if all paths start outside the closure of a dust disk $U$ and end in $U\cap B$, their last-hit locations on $\partial U$ have an atomless distribution.
  - Explanation: A path law with square-integrable occupation density visits any fixed domain point with probability zero, so last-hit locations on a dust-disk boundary have an atomless distribution.
- **claim-diffuse-intersections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-diffuse-intersections)): Fix a countable prior coordinate list satisfying <span class="upright">(P)</span>, with flag set $\Sigma$. Let $\xi$ and $\mu$ be laws on allowed tagged paths for this list, and put $A_\xi=\{s\in B:m_\xi(s)>0\}$. Assume that $$m_\xi,m_\mu\in\ell^2(B),\qquad n_\xi\|_\Sigma\in\ell^2(\Sigma),\qquad n_\mu\|_{A_\xi}\in\ell^2(A_\xi).$$ Assume also that, for each law and every slot and tag type, the corresponding tuple subprobability is absolutely continuous with respect to Haar measure on its tuple torus. Finally, assume that $\xi$ is imposed as an earlier law in the path system for $\mu$. For independent samples from $\xi$ and $\mu$, almost surely, $$\gamma_\xi([0,1])\cap\gamma_\mu([0,1])\cap B \subset \Sigma\cup\{m_\xi>0\}\cup\{m_\mu>0\}.$$ In particular all common dust visits are confined, up to a null event, to a countable set.
  - Explanation: For independent samples from an earlier and a later law, common dust visits lie almost surely in a countable set determined by the flags and hit supports.
- **claim-countable-exceptions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-countable-exceptions)): Fix any countable coordinate list satisfying <span class="upright">(P)</span>, and fix $\beta>0$. In the ungated endpoint problem with $$E=\{3R\le \|z\|\le4R\}, \qquad E_* =\{\|z\|=7R/2\},$$ there are at most countably many $b\in B$ for which no compatible real barrier $u$ is available with $$\\|\mathrm du\\|_2+\\|a_u\\|_{\ell^2(B)}<\beta.$$ Availability permits a countable enlargement of the flags. The prescribed collar sequence may start arbitrarily deep, and the barrier is required to equal one on some collar of that sequence.
  - Explanation: Only countably many boundary points can lack a small compatible barrier. Uncountably many would give laws whose forced four-path crossing contradicts the localized occupation and hit bounds.
- **claim-line-payment** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-line-payment)): Let $D$ be a finite circle domain containing infinity, with all its bounded complementary disks contained in $\{\|w\|<L\}$. Write their radii as $r_T$, allowing radius zero. Let $v$ be a smooth real function on $D$ of finite energy. Suppose its cluster values near each complementary disk lie in a bounded real interval of length at most $A(T)$.* *Fix a direction and a measurable set $J$ of transverse parameters. Suppose that on each line with parameter in $J$ there is a segment in $\{\|w\|\le L\}$ with endpoints in $D$ on which $v$ has values zero and one. Then $$\label{eq:line-payment} \|J\|\le\sqrt{\pi}\,L\\|\mathrm dv\\|_{2,D\cap\{\|w\|\le L\}} +2\sum_T r_T A(T) \le\sqrt{\pi}\,L\\|\mathrm dv\\|_2 +2L\Bigl(\sum_T A(T)^2\Bigr)^{1/2}.$$ The same first inequality holds with the gradient integrated over any measurable region containing all the chosen domain portions of the segments.
  - Explanation: The measure of transverse parameters on which lines carry a change from zero to one is bounded by the gradient energy plus the radius-weighted cluster oscillations at the round holes.
- **claim-unmarked-collapse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-unmarked-collapse)): Let $\mathscr L$ and $S$ be obtained by the preceding selection, starting with any countable compatible seed list. Extend $\mathscr L$ by any further countable compatible list, allowing further countable flag enlargements, and take a diagonal finite-transfer limit $f$ as in Section <a href="#sec:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transfer">3</a>. Then $K_b(f)$ is a singleton for every $b\in B\setminus S$.
  - Explanation: After the first selection, every component indexed outside the countable exceptional set is a point in every diagonal transfer limit, because small barrier norms contradict a positive projection length.
- **claim-law-level-intersections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-law-levels)): Fix a countable prior compatible list with flag set $\Sigma$, possibly containing period coordinates, and let $\mu$ be a law from Theorem <a href="#thm:positive-law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:positive-law">19</a>. For every prior coordinate $i$, almost surely the trace values modulo one at the free points of the path in $B\cap M_i$ form a Lebesgue-null subset of $\mathbb T$.* *Consequently, for almost every tuple defining any fixed finite prior level union $H$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{\mathbb P}}_\mu\{\gamma([0,1])\cap H\cap(B\setminus\Sigma) =\varnothing\}=1, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{\mathbb E}}_\mu\#\bigl(\gamma([0,1])\cap H\bigr)<\infty.$$ For one coordinate the integrated distinct-intersection bound is $$\label{eq:law-level-count} \int_\mathbb T\mathop{\mathrm{\mathbb E}}_\mu\#\bigl(\gamma([0,1])\cap H_i(t)\bigr)\,\mathrm dt \le \int_{G\cap M_i}g_\mu\|\mathrm du_i\|\,\mathrm da +\sum_{s\in B}m_\mu(s)a_i(s)<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Paths from a positive law meet generic prior level sets only at singleton level components at free points; trace values there form a null set and expected intersection counts are finite.
- **claim-compact-simple-arcs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-compact-arcs)): Let $\mu$ be an endpoint law supplied by Theorem <a href="#thm:positive-law" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:positive-law">19</a>, with target $b$ and prescribed collars $C_j(b)=\partial U_j(b)$. For every sufficiently deep prescribed collar $C=\partial U$, there is a probability law $\eta$ supported on a compact subset of $C([0,1],X)$ consisting of simple arcs $\alpha$ such that $$\alpha(0)\in C,\qquad \alpha(1)=b,\qquad \alpha((0,1))\subset U\setminus\{b\}.$$ Its mean domain occupation has a density $g_\eta$ satisfying $$\label{eq:arc-occupation} g_\eta\le 2\mathbf 1_{U\cap G}g_\mu \quad\text{almost everywhere}, \qquad m_\eta(s)\le 2m_\mu(s)\quad(s\in B\cap\overline U),$$ and $m_\eta=0$ outside $\overline U$. Here $m$ denotes hit probabilities, rather than visit counts. In particular, for every $\varepsilon>0$, every sufficiently deep prescribed collar admits such a law $\eta$ with $$\label{eq:arc-smallness} \\|g_\eta\\|_2+ \\|m_\eta\|_{B\setminus\{b\}}\\|_{\ell^2} <\varepsilon.$$* *For almost every tuple defining any fixed finite prior level union $H$, the expected number of distinct intersections of an arc with $H$ is finite, and the arcs almost surely have no free dust contacts in $H$. If the endpoint problem has gates, the starting point additionally satisfies $f_0(\alpha(0))\in\overline W_2$.
  - Explanation: Terminal parts of endpoint paths reduce to a compact family of simple arcs from a collar to the target, with occupation and hit probabilities at most a fixed multiple of the original and small on deep collars.
- **claim-stream-coordinate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-stream)): Let $U$ be a dust disk, let $b\in B\cap U$, and put $C=\partial U$. Suppose $\eta$ is supported on a compact family of simple arcs from $C$ to $b$, meeting $C$ only at their initial points and $b$ only at their terminal points. Assume that its mean domain occupation has an $L^2$ density $g_\eta$, that $m_\eta\in\ell^2(B)$, and that it has the generic level-intersection conclusions of Lemma <a href="#lem:compact-arcs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:compact-arcs">29</a> for the prior list.* *There is a continuous, locally Sobolev function $v$ on the cyclic cover over $G\cap\overline U$, with additive period one about $b$, such that $$\label{eq:stream-energy} \\|\mathrm dv\\|_{2,G\cap U}\le\sqrt2\,\\|g_\eta\\|_2.$$ At every $s\in B\cap U\setminus\{b\}$ its band is bounded and has width at most $m_\eta(s)$. After enlarging the flags by $\{s\in B:m_\eta(s)>0\}$, its free traces satisfy (P) against the prior list. If $\beta$ is the probability distribution of the arc starts on the positively oriented curve $C$, then $\beta$ is atomless and $$\label{eq:stream-boundary-measure} \mathrm d(v\|_C)=\beta.$$ This equality is understood on a lifted boundary interval: the branch increases by one over a full turn.* *For every $\varepsilon>0$ one may replace $v$ by a smooth coordinate $u$ of the same period such that $$\label{eq:stream-smoothing} \\|\mathrm d(u-v)\\|_{2,G\cap U}<\varepsilon, \qquad \sup_C\|u-v\|<\varepsilon,$$ with the same nonsingular bands and hence with (P). The discrepancy $u-v$ is single-valued. Thus $u$ is an admissible coordinate with mask $\overline U$ and budget $$a_u(s)=m_\eta(s)\quad(s\in B\cap U\setminus\{b\}), \qquad a_u(b)=1,$$ and zero budget off its mask.
  - Explanation: Averaging integer indices of lifted simple arcs gives a continuous period-one coordinate with energy controlled by the occupation density, bands controlled by hit probabilities, compatibility, and a boundary measure equal to the distribution of arc starts.
- **claim-stream-stokes** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-stream-stokes)): Let $u$ be a smooth period coordinate with mask $\overline U$ and singularity $b$, and put $C=\partial U$. Transfer it to a finite completion retaining $C$ and the marked hole indexed by $b$. Write $F$ for the normalized circle-domain map, $u^*$ for the transferred coordinate in the target, and $D_b$ for the distinguished round hole. Assume that the masked region lies in $\|w\|<L$. Target lengths and areas are Euclidean; the norm below is over the domain inside $F(C)$ and outside its circular holes. For a target hole $T$ inside $F(C)$, let $s_T$ be the index of its source terminal disk and $r_T$ its radius. When $s_T\ne b$, let $A_u(T)$ be its transferred boundary oscillation. Then $$\label{eq:stream-stokes} \left\|\int_{F(C)}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(w,D_b)\,\mathrm du^*\right\| \le \sqrt\pi L\,\\|\mathrm du^*\\|_2 +2\pi\sum_{T:\,s_T\ne b}r_TA_u(T).$$ Consequently, along diagonal transfers $F_n$ with errors tending to zero, write $u_n^*$ for the transferred coordinate and $D_{n,b}$ for its distinguished target disk. Then $$\label{eq:stream-stokes-asymptotic} \limsup_{n\to\infty} \left\|\int_{F_n(C)}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(w,D_{n,b})\,\mathrm du_n^*\right\| \le \sqrt\pi L\,\\|\mathrm du\\|_{2,G\cap U} +2\pi L\,\\|a_u\|_{B\setminus\{b\}}\\|_{\ell^2}.$$
  - Explanation: Stokes' theorem bounds a boundary integral of the distance to the distinguished hole for a transferred period coordinate by its energy and the radius-weighted oscillations at the other holes.
- **claim-bad-pair-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bad-pair)): Given a countable prior list satisfying (P), a mark $b$, and a bad pair $(f_0,D_0)\in\mathcal Q_b$, one can append a compatible real barrier or a compatible period coordinate singular at $b$ such that an entire relative neighborhood of $(f_0,D_0)$ is excluded as limiting pair data of diagonal finite transfers. The exclusion persists after any further countable extensions satisfying (P), provided $b$ remains marked.
  - Explanation: For a pair where the round disk is strictly smaller than the component, either a barrier or a period coordinate can be appended that excludes a neighborhood of the pair for all later diagonal transfers.
- **claim-retained-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-retained-tests)): Use the quotient sphere $X=G\cup B$, fixed exterior radius $R$, and coordinates of Definition <a href="#def:coordinates" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:coordinates">4</a>. Let $\mathscr L_0$ be any countable well-ordered list satisfying compatibility <span class="upright">(P)</span>, with a countable flag set $\Sigma_0$. Prescribe also a countable set $S_0\subset B$ of mandatory marks containing every singularity of $\mathscr L_0$.* *There are a countable well-ordered compatible extension $\mathscr L$ of $\mathscr L_0$, with enlarged countable flags, and a countable mark set $S\supset S_0$ containing all its singularities, with the following properties. Every normalized diagonal finite-transfer limit of $\mathscr L$, retaining the marks $S$, has a circle-domain image. This conclusion persists after any further countable compatible extension, retaining $S$ and including all additional singularities among the marks.* *More precisely, let $\varepsilon_n>0$ tend to zero and let $H_n\subset G$ be increasing compact sets such that every compact subset of $G$ is contained in $\operatorname{int}H_n$ for all sufficiently large $n$. One can choose increasing finite subsets $I_n$ exhausting the labels of $\mathscr L$, finite bordered completions $(Q_n,g_n)$, nested smooth cores $K_n\subset G$, transferred coordinates $u_{i,n}$, and normalized conformal maps $$f_n:Q_n^\circ\longrightarrow\Omega_n, \qquad f_n(z)=z+O(1/z),$$ where $\Omega_n$ is a finite circle domain, such that $H_n\subset \operatorname{int}K_n$ and the following assertions hold.* 1. *The metric $g_n$ is the original spherical metric near $K_n$. For every $i\in I_n$, the coordinate $u_{i,n}$ agrees with $u_i$ near $K_n\cap M_i$, and all its terminal periods are those specified in Theorem <a href="#thm:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:transfer">8</a>.* 2. *The total additional energy satisfies $$\label{eq:retained-total-tail} \sum_{i\in I_n} \\|\mathrm du_{i,n}\\|_{2,(Q_n\setminus K_n)\cap M_i,g_n}^2 <\varepsilon_n^2.$$ For every $i\in I_n$, if $T_{n,s}\subset M_i$ is a terminal disk and $s$ is nonsingular for $u_i$, its boundary oscillation $A_{i,n}(T_{n,s})$ satisfies $$\label{eq:retained-excess-oscillation} \left(\sum_{\substack{s:\ T_{n,s}\subset M_i\\s\ne p_i}} [A_{i,n}(T_{n,s})-a_i(s)]_+^2\right)^{1/2} <\varepsilon_n.$$ The singularity exclusion is omitted for real coordinates.* 3. *Every prescribed mark eventually indexes a terminal disk. All terminal disks have quotient diameter tending uniformly to zero. After passing to a subsequence, $f_n\to f$ locally uniformly on $G$, where $f$ is normalized and univalent and $f(G)$ is a circle domain. At each marked end, its distinguished target disks converge to the entire component $K_b(f)$.* 4. *All complementary disks of $\Omega_n$, and all images of physical points in $Q_n^\circ$ with $\|z\|\le4R$, lie in $\|w\|<5R$. Any prescribed compact source sets, including finitely many smooth contours and their collars at each stage, can be retained in $K_n$. A retained oriented dust contour has the bounded-side correspondence of Lemma <a href="#lem:end-components" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:end-components">10</a>. For each fixed such contour its bounded target sides lie in a fixed disk, uniformly in $n$ and in the number of retained tests.* *The finite subsets $I_n$ carry their inherited order from $\mathscr L$; they need not be initial segments of its well-order. The original coordinates, their masks, and their budgets are unchanged.
  - Explanation: Any countable compatible seed list extends, with a countable mark set, so that every diagonal finite-transfer limit has a circle-domain image, with exact core agreement and vanishing excess energy and oscillation, persisting under further extensions.
- **claim-scalar-retained-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-scalar-retained-tests)): Let $G\subset\widehat{\mathbb C}$ contain infinity, let $X=G\cup B$ be its quotient sphere, and fix $R$ with $\{\|z\|\ge R\}\cup\{\infty\}\subset G$. Let $u_1,\ldots,u_m\in C(X,\mathbb R)$ be smooth on $G$, with $\int_G\|\mathrm du_i\|^2<\infty$, and suppose $$\label{eq:scalar-pair-null} \mathop{\mathrm{area}}\bigl((u_i,u_j)(B)\bigr)=0\qquad(i\ne j).$$ For every positive sequence $\varepsilon_n\to0$ there are finite bordered completions $(Q_n,g_n)$, nested smooth cores $K_n\subset G$ containing every compact subset of $G$ in their interiors eventually, normalized conformal maps $f_n:Q_n^\circ\to\Omega_n$ onto finite circle domains, and smooth real tests $v_{i,n}$ on $\Omega_n$ such that $$\begin{aligned} v_{i,n}\circ f_n&=u_i &&\text{near }K_n,\label{eq:scalar-core}\\ \sum_{i=1}^m\int_{\Omega_n\setminus f_n(K_n)} \|\nabla v_{i,n}\|^2\,\mathrm da &<\varepsilon_n^2,\label{eq:scalar-tail}\\ \sum_T A_{i,n}(T)^2&<\varepsilon_n^2 &&(1\le i\le m).\label{eq:scalar-oscillation} \end{aligned}$$ Here $T$ ranges over the complementary round disks of $\Omega_n$; the cluster values of $v_{i,n}$ at $T$ lie in an interval of length $A_{i,n}(T)$. The metric $g_n$ is the original metric near $K_n$. After passage to a subsequence, $f_n\to f$ locally uniformly on $G$, where $f$ is normalized and univalent and $f(G)$ is a circle domain. All finite target holes lie in $\|w\|<5R$. The source-contour retention, correspondence, and bounds of Theorem <a href="#thm:retained-tests" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:retained-tests">33</a><span class="upright">(iv)</span> also hold. In particular, these conclusions allow arbitrarily many, and possibly uncountably many, point components in the limiting complement.
  - Explanation: For finitely many continuous real tests whose pairwise boundary images are planar null sets, finite circle-domain approximations retain the tests on cores with vanishing tail energy and oscillation sums, and converge to a circle domain.
- **claim-hyperbolic-hull-realization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-hyperbolic)): Let $S$ be a connected orientable surface without boundary, of genus zero, and let $g$ be a complete Riemannian metric on $S$ with Gaussian curvature identically $-1$. There is a compact set $Y\subset\partial_\infty\mathbb H^3$, containing at least three points, whose complement is nonempty and connected, such that every connected component of $Y$ is a closed round disk or a singleton and $(S,d_g)$ is isometric to $\partial C(Y)$ with its intrinsic path metric.* *When $C(Y)$ is two-dimensional, $\partial C(Y)$ denotes the metric double of $C(Y)$ across its relative boundary in the containing totally geodesic plane. Surfaces are understood to be Hausdorff and second countable; their number of ends is unrestricted.
  - Explanation: Every complete genus-zero hyperbolic surface is intrinsically isometric to the boundary of the hyperbolic convex hull of a compact set in the ideal sphere whose components are round disks or points.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $u_1,\\ldots,u_m$ be a finite ordered list satisfying Definition <a href=\"#def:coordinates\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:coordinates\">4</a> and compatibility (P), with a common countable flag set $\\Sigma$. The same assertion holds for a finite ordered sublist of a countable compatible list. Prescribe a finite set $F\\subset B$ containing all singular points of the list, a compact core $K_0\\subset G$, a dust-disk cover from the fixed exhaustion, and $\\varepsilon>0$.*\n\n*There exist a smooth compact core $K\\subset G$, finitely many terminal disks $T_s$ with pairwise disjoint closures, indexed by a finite set $S\\subset B$ containing $F$, and a smooth metric $g$ on the physical sphere, with the following properties. Every terminal disk lies in a member of the prescribed cover, $s\\in T_s$, and $K$ contains $K_0$ and all mask boundaries in its interior. The metric $g$ agrees with the spherical metric on a neighborhood of $K$. Write $$Q=\\widehat{\\mathbb C}\\setminus\\bigcup_{s\\in S}T_s,\n \\qquad Q^\\circ=\\widehat{\\mathbb C}\\setminus\\bigcup_{s\\in S}\\overline{T_s}.$$ Then $K\\subset Q^\\circ$, and $Q$, equipped with $g$, is a smooth bordered surface of genus zero. Each $u_i$ has a transferred coordinate $u_i^*$ on $Q\\cap M_i$, smooth up to its boundary in local real branches, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *$u_i^*=u_i$ on a neighborhood of $K\\cap M_i$;*\n\n\n2.  *The total energy outside $K$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:transfer-tail}\n     \\sum_{i=1}^m\n     \\|\\mathrm du_i^*\\|_{2,(Q\\setminus K)\\cap M_i,g}^2<\\varepsilon^2;$$*\n\n\n3.  *For each nonsingular terminal index $s\\in S\\cap M_i$, a single-valued lift on a collar of $\\partial T_s$ has boundary oscillation $A_i(T_s)$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:transfer-oscillation}\n     \\left(\\sum_{\\substack{s\\in S\\cap M_i\\\\s\\ne p_i}}\n           [A_i(T_s)-a_i(s)]_+^2\\right)^{1/2}<\\varepsilon.$$ Here $[x]_+=\\max\\{x,0\\}$. For a real coordinate the restriction $s\\ne p_i$ is omitted.*\n\n4.  *A periodic coordinate still adds $1$ under its positive deck translation. Its differential has period $1$ around the terminal disk indexed by its singular point, and period $0$ around every other terminal disk in its mask. Here a positive circuit around a terminal disk is counterclockwise around that disk.*\n\n*The core $K_0$ may contain any prescribed compact subsets of $G$. In particular the conformal structure on $\\{|z|\\ge R\\}$ can always be retained. Terminal disks can be required to lie in arbitrarily small prescribed dust neighborhoods of their indices.",
        "source_id": "PM-071.2"

For a real coordinate the restriction $s\\ne p_i$ is omitted.*\n\n4.  *A periodic coordinate still adds $1$ under its positive deck translation. Its differential has period $1$ around the terminal disk indexed by its singular point, and period $0$ around every other terminal disk in its mask. Here a positive circuit around a terminal disk is counterclockwise around that disk.*\n\n*The core $K_0$ may contain any prescribed compact subsets of $G$. In particular the conformal structure on $\\{|z|\\ge R\\}$ can always be retained. Terminal disks can be required to lie in arbitrarily small prescribed dust neighborhoods of their indices.",
      "explanation": "A finite compatible list of coordinates transfers to a smooth bordered genus-zero surface. The coordinates are unchanged on a prescribed core, with arbitrarily small added energy and excess boundary oscillation, and periods are preserved exactly.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-normalized-limits",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $L$, depending only on $R$, with the following properties. For every completion just described, all complementary disks of its normalized circle domain and all images of physical points in its domain with $|z|\\le4R$ lie in $\\{|w|<L\\}$. One may take $L=5R$. After passage to a subsequence, $f_n$ converges locally uniformly on $G$ to a normalized univalent map $f$. Simultaneously, for every fixed mark $b$, the disks $D_{n,b}$ converge in the Hausdorff metric to a closed round disk $D_b$, possibly a singleton.*\n\n*The same bound $L$ holds for the bounded complementary components and the images of $G\\cap\\{|z|\\le4R\\}$ for every normalized univalent map on $G$.",
        "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $f$ be a normalized univalent map on $G$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-path-stability",
        "exact_quote": "Allowed paths are stable under reversal, affine reparametrization, and restriction to a closed parameter subinterval. More generally, suppose a continuous path is made from countably many restrictions of allowed paths, possibly reversed, on disjoint open parameter intervals.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-path-coding",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed path-system data, the allowed tagged codes form a standard Borel space. On this space the domain-part length and the arclength occupation measure are Borel.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-collar-splicing",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a path system and a metric $(g,m)$ with $g\\ge c_0>0$ on $G$. Let allowed paths $\\gamma_n$, whose endpoints are in $G$, have costs converging to a finite number $A$.*\n\n1.  *If their initial and final endpoints converge to distinct points $p,q\\in B$, then for every $\\eta>0$ there is an allowed path $\\gamma$ from $p$ to $q$ satisfying $$\\mathcal L^\\circ_{g,m}(\\gamma)\\le A+\\eta.$$*\n\n2.  *If their initial endpoints belong to a set $F\\subset G$ and their final endpoints converge to $p\\in B\\setminus\\overline F$, where closure is in $X$, there is an allowed path from a point of $F$ to $p$ with $\\mathcal L^-_{g,m}\\le A+\\eta$.*\n\n*The paths produced consist of countably many restricted original pieces and joining arcs on smooth collar curves in $G$. Every dust point avoided by the original pieces remains avoided away from the newly adjoined endpoint times. Any prescribed hit restrictions are also preserved when the joining collars are disjoint from their hit curves.",
        "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-positive-law",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that, for some $\\beta>0$, there is no new compatible barrier coordinate with $$\\|\\mathrm du\\|_{2,G\\setminus O}+\\|a_u\\|_{\\ell^2(B)}<\\beta.$$ Nonexistence here allows an arbitrary countable enlargement of the flags and, in the gated problem, any penalty region permitted in Definition <a href=\"#def:endpoint\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:endpoint\">11</a>. Then there is a probability law $\\mu$ on base codes with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\bar\\ell_\\mu=g_\\mu\\,\\mathrm da$, where $g_\\mu\\in L^2(G,\\mathrm da)$;*\n\n2.  *$n_\\mu|_{D_*}\\in\\ell^2(D_*)$ and $m_\\mu\\in\\ell^2(B)$;*\n\n3.  *for every slot and every tag type, its tuple distribution, regarded as a subprobability measure, is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure on the corresponding tuple torus.*\n\n*The three norms in <span class=\"upright\">(i)–(ii)</span> are bounded by a constant depending only on $\\beta$ and the numerical constant in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:barrier\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:barrier\">13</a>.         "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-positive-law",

Then there is a probability law $\\mu$ on base codes with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\bar\\ell_\\mu=g_\\mu\\,\\mathrm da$, where $g_\\mu\\in L^2(G,\\mathrm da)$;*\n\n2.  *$n_\\mu|_{D_*}\\in\\ell^2(D_*)$ and $m_\\mu\\in\\ell^2(B)$;*\n\n3.  *for every slot and every tag type, its tuple distribution, regarded as a subprobability measure, is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure on the corresponding tuple torus.*\n\n*The three norms in <span class=\"upright\">(i)–(ii)</span> are bounded by a constant depending only on $\\beta$ and the numerical constant in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:barrier\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:barrier\">13</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-modulus-duality",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be Polish, let $\\sigma$ be a finite Borel measure on $Y$, let $A$ be standard Borel, and let $a\\mapsto\\nu_a\\in\\mathcal M_+(Y)$ be Borel. If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Mod}}_\\sigma(A)>c>0$, then, for every $C>c^{-1/2}$, there is a Borel probability measure $P$ on $A$ whose barycenter satisfies $$\\int_A\\nu_a\\,\\mathrm dP(a)=q\\,\\sigma,\n \\qquad q\\ge0,\n \\qquad \\|q\\|_{L^2(\\sigma)}\\le C.$$ Moreover, after giving $A$ a Polish topology with its original Borel sets and with $a\\mapsto\\nu_a$ continuous, the law may be supported on a compact subset of $A$.",
        "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-modulus-duality",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be Polish, let $\\sigma$ be a finite Borel measure on $Y$, let $A$ be standard Borel, and let $a\\mapsto\\nu_a\\in\\mathcal M_+(Y)$ be Borel.           "source_id": "PM-071.2"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-countable-law",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypothesis of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:positive-law\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:positive-law\">19</a>, there is a constant $C<\\infty$, depending only on $\\beta$ and the constant in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:barrier\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:barrier\">13</a>, with the following property.         "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },

      "exact_statement": "Under the hypothesis of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:positive-law\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:positive-law\">19</a>, there is a constant $C<\\infty$, depending only on $\\beta$ and the constant in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:barrier\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:barrier\">13</a>, with the following property.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-law-fusion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be standard Borel, let $B$ be compact metric, and let $\\mathcal M$ be a Borel family of probability measures on $S$, closed under Borel probability mixtures. Suppose a Borel relation $\\mathcal H\\subset S\\times B$ defines $$m_z(s)=z\\{x\\in S:(x,s)\\in\\mathcal H\\}\\in[0,1].$$ Assume that $\\mathcal M$ has a Polish topology generating its Borel sets for which $(z,s)\\mapsto m_z(s)$ is jointly upper semicontinuous. Finally, suppose that for every countable $D\\subset B$ and every finite atomless Borel measure $\\lambda$ on $B$, some $z\\in\\mathcal M$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:fusion-countable-tests}\n \\|m_z|_D\\|_{\\ell^2(D)}\\le C_0,\n \\qquad \\int_Bm_z\\,\\mathrm d\\lambda=0.$$ Then, for every $\\varepsilon>0$, there exists $\\bar z\\in\\mathcal M$ with $\\|m_{\\bar z}\\|_{\\ell^2(B)}\\le C_0+\\varepsilon$.",
        "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-law-fusion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be standard Borel, let $B$ be compact metric, and let $\\mathcal M$ be a Borel family of probability measures on $S$, closed under Borel probability mixtures.           "source_id": "PM-071.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-diffuse-intersections",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a countable prior coordinate list satisfying <span class=\"upright\">(P)</span>, with flag set $\\Sigma$. Let $\\xi$ and $\\mu$ be laws on allowed tagged paths for this list, and put $A_\\xi=\\{s\\in B:m_\\xi(s)>0\\}$. Assume that $$m_\\xi,m_\\mu\\in\\ell^2(B),\\qquad\n n_\\xi|_\\Sigma\\in\\ell^2(\\Sigma),\\qquad\n n_\\mu|_{A_\\xi}\\in\\ell^2(A_\\xi).$$ Assume also that, for each law and every slot and tag type, the corresponding tuple subprobability is absolutely continuous with respect to Haar measure on its tuple torus. Finally, assume that $\\xi$ is imposed as an earlier law in the path system for $\\mu$.         "anchor": "stmt-thm-countable-exceptions",
        "exact_quote": "Fix any countable coordinate list satisfying <span class=\"upright\">(P)</span>, and fix $\\beta>0$. In the ungated endpoint problem with $$E=\\{3R\\le |z|\\le4R\\},\n \\qquad E_* =\\{|z|=7R/2\\},$$ there are at most countably many $b\\in B$ for which no compatible real barrier $u$ is available with $$\\|\\mathrm du\\|_2+\\|a_u\\|_{\\ell^2(B)}<\\beta.$$ Availability permits a countable enlargement of the flags. The prescribed collar sequence may start arbitrarily deep, and the barrier is required to equal one on some collar of that sequence.",
        "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-071.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-line-payment",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a finite circle domain containing infinity, with all its bounded complementary disks contained in $\\{|w|<L\\}$. Write their radii as $r_T$, allowing radius zero. Let $v$ be a smooth real function on $D$ of finite energy. Suppose its cluster values near each complementary disk lie in a bounded real interval of length at most $A(T)$.*\n\n\n*Fix a direction and a measurable set $J$ of transverse parameters. Suppose that on each line with parameter in $J$ there is a segment in $\\{|w|\\le L\\}$ with endpoints in $D$ on which $v$ has values zero and one. Then $$\\label{eq:line-payment}\n |J|\\le\\sqrt{\\pi}\\,L\\|\\mathrm dv\\|_{2,D\\cap\\{|w|\\le L\\}}\n       +2\\sum_T r_T A(T)\n \\le\\sqrt{\\pi}\\,L\\|\\mathrm dv\\|_2\n       +2L\\Bigl(\\sum_T A(T)^2\\Bigr)^{1/2}.$$ The same first inequality holds with the gradient integrated over any measurable region containing all the chosen domain portions of the segments.",
        "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-line-payment",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a finite circle domain containing infinity, with all its bounded complementary disks contained in $\\{|w|<L\\}$. Write their radii as $r_T$, allowing radius zero. Suppose that on each line with parameter in $J$ there is a segment in $\\{|w|\\le L\\}$ with endpoints in $D$ on which $v$ has values zero and one. Then $$\\label{eq:line-payment}\n |J|\\le\\sqrt{\\pi}\\,L\\|\\mathrm dv\\|_{2,D\\cap\\{|w|\\le L\\}}\n       +2\\sum_T r_T A(T)\n \\le\\sqrt{\\pi}\\,L\\|\\mathrm dv\\|_2\n       +2L\\Bigl(\\sum_T A(T)^2\\Bigr)^{1/2}.$$ The same first inequality holds with the gradient integrated over any measurable region containing all the chosen domain portions of the segments.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathscr L$ and $S$ be obtained by the preceding selection, starting with any countable compatible seed list.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-law-levels",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a countable prior compatible list with flag set $\\Sigma$, possibly containing period coordinates, and let $\\mu$ be a law from Theorem <a href=\"#thm:positive-law\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:positive-law\">19</a>. For every prior coordinate $i$, almost surely the trace values modulo one at the free points of the path in $B\\cap M_i$ form a Lebesgue-null subset of $\\mathbb T$.*\n\n\n*Consequently, for almost every tuple defining any fixed finite prior level union $H$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{\\mathbb P}}_\\mu\\{\\gamma([0,1])\\cap H\\cap(B\\setminus\\Sigma)\n                         =\\varnothing\\}=1,\n \\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{\\mathbb E}}_\\mu\\#\\bigl(\\gamma([0,1])\\cap H\\bigr)<\\infty.$$ For one coordinate the integrated distinct-intersection bound is $$\\label{eq:law-level-count}\n \\int_\\mathbb T\\mathop{\\mathrm{\\mathbb E}}_\\mu\\#\\bigl(\\gamma([0,1])\\cap H_i(t)\\bigr)\\,\\mathrm dt\n \\le \\int_{G\\cap M_i}g_\\mu|\\mathrm du_i|\\,\\mathrm da\n       +\\sum_{s\\in B}m_\\mu(s)a_i(s)<\\infty.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-law-levels",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a countable prior compatible list with flag set $\\Sigma$, possibly containing period coordinates, and let $\\mu$ be a law from Theorem <a href=\"#thm:positive-law\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:positive-law\">19</a>.           "source_id": "PM-071.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix a countable prior compatible list with flag set $\\Sigma$, possibly containing period coordinates, and let $\\mu$ be a law from Theorem <a href=\"#thm:positive-law\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:positive-law\">19</a>.       "explanation": "Paths from a positive law meet generic prior level sets only at singleton level components at free points; trace values there form a null set and expected intersection counts are finite.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-compact-arcs",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be an endpoint law supplied by Theorem <a href=\"#thm:positive-law\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:positive-law\">19</a>, with target $b$ and prescribed collars $C_j(b)=\\partial U_j(b)$. For every sufficiently deep prescribed collar $C=\\partial U$, there is a probability law $\\eta$ supported on a compact subset of $C([0,1],X)$ consisting of simple arcs $\\alpha$ such that $$\\alpha(0)\\in C,\\qquad \\alpha(1)=b,\\qquad\n \\alpha((0,1))\\subset U\\setminus\\{b\\}.$$ Its mean domain occupation has a density $g_\\eta$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:arc-occupation}\n g_\\eta\\le 2\\mathbf 1_{U\\cap G}g_\\mu\n \\quad\\text{almost everywhere},\n \\qquad\n m_\\eta(s)\\le 2m_\\mu(s)\\quad(s\\in B\\cap\\overline U),$$ and $m_\\eta=0$ outside $\\overline U$. Here $m$ denotes hit probabilities, rather than visit counts. In particular, for every $\\varepsilon>0$, every sufficiently deep prescribed collar admits such a law $\\eta$ with $$\\label{eq:arc-smallness}\n \\|g_\\eta\\|_2+\n \\|m_\\eta|_{B\\setminus\\{b\\}}\\|_{\\ell^2}\n <\\varepsilon.$$*\n\n*For almost every tuple defining any fixed finite prior level union $H$, the expected number of distinct intersections of an arc with $H$ is finite, and the arcs almost surely have no free dust contacts in $H$. If the endpoint problem has gates, the starting point additionally satisfies $f_0(\\alpha(0))\\in\\overline W_2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },

Here $m$ denotes hit probabilities, rather than visit counts. If the endpoint problem has gates, the starting point additionally satisfies $f_0(\\alpha(0))\\in\\overline W_2$.",
      "explanation": "Terminal parts of endpoint paths reduce to a compact family of simple arcs from a collar to the target, with occupation and hit probabilities at most a fixed multiple of the original and small on deep collars.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-stream",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a dust disk, let $b\\in B\\cap U$, and put $C=\\partial U$. Suppose $\\eta$ is supported on a compact family of simple arcs from $C$ to $b$, meeting $C$ only at their initial points and $b$ only at their terminal points. Assume that its mean domain occupation has an $L^2$ density $g_\\eta$, that $m_\\eta\\in\\ell^2(B)$, and that it has the generic level-intersection conclusions of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:compact-arcs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:compact-arcs\">29</a> for the prior list.*\n\n\n\n*There is a continuous, locally Sobolev function $v$ on the cyclic cover over $G\\cap\\overline U$, with additive period one about $b$, such that $$\\label{eq:stream-energy}\n \\|\\mathrm dv\\|_{2,G\\cap U}\\le\\sqrt2\\,\\|g_\\eta\\|_2.$$ At every $s\\in B\\cap U\\setminus\\{b\\}$ its band is bounded and has width at most $m_\\eta(s)$. After enlarging the flags by $\\{s\\in B:m_\\eta(s)>0\\}$, its free traces satisfy (P) against the prior list. If $\\beta$ is the probability distribution of the arc starts on the positively oriented curve $C$, then $\\beta$ is atomless and $$\\label{eq:stream-boundary-measure}\n \\mathrm d(v|_C)=\\beta.$$ This equality is understood on a lifted boundary interval: the branch increases by one over a full turn.*\n\n\n*For every $\\varepsilon>0$ one may replace $v$ by a smooth coordinate $u$ of the same period such that $$\\label{eq:stream-smoothing}\n \\|\\mathrm d(u-v)\\|_{2,G\\cap U}<\\varepsilon,\n \\qquad \\sup_C|u-v|<\\varepsilon,$$ with the same nonsingular bands and hence with (P). The discrepancy $u-v$ is single-valued. Thus $u$ is an admissible coordinate with mask $\\overline U$ and budget $$a_u(s)=m_\\eta(s)\\quad(s\\in B\\cap U\\setminus\\{b\\}),\n \\qquad a_u(b)=1,$$ and zero budget off its mask.",
        "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-stream",
          "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be a dust disk, let $b\\in B\\cap U$, and put $C=\\partial U$. Assume that its mean domain occupation has an $L^2$ density $g_\\eta$, that $m_\\eta\\in\\ell^2(B)$, and that it has the generic level-intersection conclusions of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:compact-arcs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:compact-arcs\">29</a> for the prior list.*\n\n\n\n*There is a continuous, locally Sobolev function $v$ on the cyclic cover over $G\\cap\\overline U$, with additive period one about $b$, such that $$\\label{eq:stream-energy}\n \\|\\mathrm dv\\|_{2,G\\cap U}\\le\\sqrt2\\,\\|g_\\eta\\|_2.$$ At every $s\\in B\\cap U\\setminus\\{b\\}$ its band is bounded and has width at most $m_\\eta(s)$. After enlarging the flags by $\\{s\\in B:m_\\eta(s)>0\\}$, its free traces satisfy (P) against the prior list. If $\\beta$ is the probability distribution of the arc starts on the positively oriented curve $C$, then $\\beta$ is atomless and $$\\label{eq:stream-boundary-measure}\n \\mathrm d(v|_C)=\\beta.$$ This equality is understood on a lifted boundary interval: the branch increases by one over a full turn.*\n\n\n*For every $\\varepsilon>0$ one may replace $v$ by a smooth coordinate $u$ of the same period such that $$\\label{eq:stream-smoothing}\n \\|\\mathrm d(u-v)\\|_{2,G\\cap U}<\\varepsilon,\n \\qquad \\sup_C|u-v|<\\varepsilon,$$ with the same nonsingular bands and hence with (P). The discrepancy $u-v$ is single-valued. Thus $u$ is an admissible coordinate with mask $\\overline U$ and budget $$a_u(s)=m_\\eta(s)\\quad(s\\in B\\cap U\\setminus\\{b\\}),\n \\qquad a_u(b)=1,$$ and zero budget off its mask.",
          "source_id": "PM-071.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $U$ be a dust disk, let $b\\in B\\cap U$, and put $C=\\partial U$. The discrepancy $u-v$ is single-valued. Thus $u$ is an admissible coordinate with mask $\\overline U$ and budget $$a_u(s)=m_\\eta(s)\\quad(s\\in B\\cap U\\setminus\\{b\\}),\n \\qquad a_u(b)=1,$$ and zero budget off its mask.",
      "explanation": "Averaging integer indices of lifted simple arcs gives a continuous period-one coordinate with energy controlled by the occupation density, bands controlled by hit probabilities, compatibility, and a boundary measure equal to the distribution of arc starts.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-stream-stokes",
        "exact_quote": "Let $u$ be a smooth period coordinate with mask $\\overline U$ and singularity $b$, and put $C=\\partial U$. Transfer it to a finite completion retaining $C$ and the marked hole indexed by $b$. Write $F$ for the normalized circle-domain map, $u^*$ for the transferred coordinate in the target, and $D_b$ for the distinguished round hole. Assume that the masked region lies in $|w|<L$. Target lengths and areas are Euclidean; the norm below is over the domain inside $F(C)$ and outside its circular holes. For a target hole $T$ inside $F(C)$, let $s_T$ be the index of its source terminal disk and $r_T$ its radius. When $s_T\\ne b$, let $A_u(T)$ be its transferred boundary oscillation. Then $$\\label{eq:stream-stokes}\n \\left|\\int_{F(C)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(w,D_b)\\,\\mathrm du^*\\right|\n \\le \\sqrt\\pi L\\,\\|\\mathrm du^*\\|_2\n       +2\\pi\\sum_{T:\\,s_T\\ne b}r_TA_u(T).$$ Consequently, along diagonal transfers $F_n$ with errors tending to zero, write $u_n^*$ for the transferred coordinate and $D_{n,b}$ for its distinguished target disk. Then $$\\label{eq:stream-stokes-asymptotic}\n \\limsup_{n\\to\\infty}\n \\left|\\int_{F_n(C)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(w,D_{n,b})\\,\\mathrm du_n^*\\right|\n \\le \\sqrt\\pi L\\,\\|\\mathrm du\\|_{2,G\\cap U}\n       +2\\pi L\\,\\|a_u|_{B\\setminus\\{b\\}}\\|_{\\ell^2}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-stream-stokes",
          "exact_quote": "Let $u$ be a smooth period coordinate with mask $\\overline U$ and singularity $b$, and put $C=\\partial U$. Transfer it to a finite completion retaining $C$ and the marked hole indexed by $b$. Write $F$ for the normalized circle-domain map, $u^*$ for the transferred coordinate in the target, and $D_b$ for the distinguished round hole. Assume that the masked region lies in $|w|<L$. Target lengths and areas are Euclidean; the norm below is over the domain inside $F(C)$ and outside its circular holes. For a target hole $T$ inside $F(C)$, let $s_T$ be the index of its source terminal disk and $r_T$ its radius. When $s_T\\ne b$, let $A_u(T)$ be its transferred boundary oscillation. Then $$\\label{eq:stream-stokes}\n \\left|\\int_{F(C)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(w,D_b)\\,\\mathrm du^*\\right|\n \\le \\sqrt\\pi L\\,\\|\\mathrm du^*\\|_2\n       +2\\pi\\sum_{T:\\,s_T\\ne b}r_TA_u(T).$$ Consequently, along diagonal transfers $F_n$ with errors tending to zero, write $u_n^*$ for the transferred coordinate and $D_{n,b}$ for its distinguished target disk. Then $$\\label{eq:stream-stokes-asymptotic}\n \\limsup_{n\\to\\infty}\n \\left|\\int_{F_n(C)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(w,D_{n,b})\\,\\mathrm du_n^*\\right|\n \\le \\sqrt\\pi L\\,\\|\\mathrm du\\|_{2,G\\cap U}\n       +2\\pi L\\,\\|a_u|_{B\\setminus\\{b\\}}\\|_{\\ell^2}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-071.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $u$ be a smooth period coordinate with mask $\\overline U$ and singularity $b$, and put $C=\\partial U$. Transfer it to a finite completion retaining $C$ and the marked hole indexed by $b$. Write $F$ for the normalized circle-domain map, $u^*$ for the transferred coordinate in the target, and $D_b$ for the distinguished round hole. Assume that the masked region lies in $|w|<L$. Target lengths and areas are Euclidean; the norm below is over the domain inside $F(C)$ and outside its circular holes. For a target hole $T$ inside $F(C)$, let $s_T$ be the index of its source terminal disk and $r_T$ its radius. When $s_T\\ne b$, let $A_u(T)$ be its transferred boundary oscillation. Then $$\\label{eq:stream-stokes}\n \\left|\\int_{F(C)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(w,D_b)\\,\\mathrm du^*\\right|\n \\le \\sqrt\\pi L\\,\\|\\mathrm du^*\\|_2\n       +2\\pi\\sum_{T:\\,s_T\\ne b}r_TA_u(T).$$ Consequently, along diagonal transfers $F_n$ with errors tending to zero, write $u_n^*$ for the transferred coordinate and $D_{n,b}$ for its distinguished target disk. Then $$\\label{eq:stream-stokes-asymptotic}\n \\limsup_{n\\to\\infty}\n \\left|\\int_{F_n(C)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(w,D_{n,b})\\,\\mathrm du_n^*\\right|\n \\le \\sqrt\\pi L\\,\\|\\mathrm du\\|_{2,G\\cap U}\n       +2\\pi L\\,\\|a_u|_{B\\setminus\\{b\\}}\\|_{\\ell^2}.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bad-pair",
        "exact_quote": "Given a countable prior list satisfying (P), a mark $b$, and a bad pair $(f_0,D_0)\\in\\mathcal Q_b$, one can append a compatible real barrier or a compatible period coordinate singular at $b$ such that an entire relative neighborhood of $(f_0,D_0)$ is excluded as limiting pair data of diagonal finite transfers. The exclusion persists after any further countable extensions satisfying (P), provided $b$ remains marked.",
        "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-071.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Given a countable prior list satisfying (P), a mark $b$, and a bad pair $(f_0,D_0)\\in\\mathcal Q_b$, one can append a compatible real barrier or a compatible period coordinate singular at $b$ such that an entire relative neighborhood of $(f_0,D_0)$ is excluded as limiting pair data of diagonal finite transfers.       "explanation": "For a pair where the round disk is strictly smaller than the component, either a barrier or a period coordinate can be appended that excludes a neighborhood of the pair for all later diagonal transfers.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-retained-tests",
        "exact_quote": "Use the quotient sphere $X=G\\cup B$, fixed exterior radius $R$, and coordinates of Definition <a href=\"#def:coordinates\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:coordinates\">4</a>. Let $\\mathscr L_0$ be any countable well-ordered list satisfying compatibility <span class=\"upright\">(P)</span>, with a countable flag set $\\Sigma_0$. Prescribe also a countable set $S_0\\subset B$ of mandatory marks containing every singularity of $\\mathscr L_0$.*\n\n*There are a countable well-ordered compatible extension $\\mathscr L$ of $\\mathscr L_0$, with enlarged countable flags, and a countable mark set $S\\supset S_0$ containing all its singularities, with the following properties. Every normalized diagonal finite-transfer limit of $\\mathscr L$, retaining the marks $S$, has a circle-domain image. This conclusion persists after any further countable compatible extension, retaining $S$ and including all additional singularities among the marks.*\n\n*More precisely, let $\\varepsilon_n>0$ tend to zero and let $H_n\\subset G$ be increasing compact sets such that every compact subset of $G$ is contained in $\\operatorname{int}H_n$ for all sufficiently large $n$. One can choose increasing finite subsets $I_n$ exhausting the labels of $\\mathscr L$, finite bordered completions $(Q_n,g_n)$, nested smooth cores $K_n\\subset G$, transferred coordinates $u_{i,n}$, and normalized conformal maps $$f_n:Q_n^\\circ\\longrightarrow\\Omega_n,\n \\qquad f_n(z)=z+O(1/z),$$ where $\\Omega_n$ is a finite circle domain, such that $H_n\\subset\n\\operatorname{int}K_n$ and the following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *The metric $g_n$ is the original spherical metric near $K_n$. For every $i\\in I_n$, the coordinate $u_{i,n}$ agrees with $u_i$ near $K_n\\cap M_i$, and all its terminal periods are those specified in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:transfer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:transfer\">8</a>.*\n\n\n2.  *The total additional energy satisfies $$\\label{eq:retained-total-tail}\n     \\sum_{i\\in I_n}\n     \\|\\mathrm du_{i,n}\\|_{2,(Q_n\\setminus K_n)\\cap M_i,g_n}^2\n     <\\varepsilon_n^2.$$ For every $i\\in I_n$, if $T_{n,s}\\subset M_i$ is a terminal disk and $s$ is nonsingular for $u_i$, its boundary oscillation $A_{i,n}(T_{n,s})$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:retained-excess-oscillation}\n     \\left(\\sum_{\\substack{s:\\ T_{n,s}\\subset M_i\\\\s\\ne p_i}}\n     [A_{i,n}(T_{n,s})-a_i(s)]_+^2\\right)^{1/2}\n     <\\varepsilon_n.$$ The singularity exclusion is omitted for real coordinates.*\n\n3.  *Every prescribed mark eventually indexes a terminal disk. All terminal disks have quotient diameter tending uniformly to zero. After passing to a subsequence, $f_n\\to f$ locally uniformly on $G$, where $f$ is normalized and univalent and $f(G)$ is a circle domain. At each marked end, its distinguished target disks converge to the entire component $K_b(f)$.*\n\n4.  *All complementary disks of $\\Omega_n$, and all images of physical points in $Q_n^\\circ$ with $|z|\\le4R$, lie in $|w|<5R$. Any prescribed compact source sets, including finitely many smooth contours and their collars at each stage, can be retained in $K_n$. A retained oriented dust contour has the bounded-side correspondence of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:end-components\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:end-components\">10</a>. For each fixed such contour its bounded target sides lie in a fixed disk, uniformly in $n$ and in the number of retained tests.*\n\n*The finite subsets $I_n$ carry their inherited order from $\\mathscr L$; they need not be initial segments of its well-order. The original coordinates, their masks, and their budgets are unchanged.",
        "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-scalar-retained-tests",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G\\subset\\widehat{\\mathbb C}$ contain infinity, let $X=G\\cup B$ be its quotient sphere, and fix $R$ with $\\{|z|\\ge R\\}\\cup\\{\\infty\\}\\subset G$. Let $u_1,\\ldots,u_m\\in C(X,\\mathbb R)$ be smooth on $G$, with $\\int_G|\\mathrm du_i|^2<\\infty$, and suppose $$\\label{eq:scalar-pair-null}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{area}}\\bigl((u_i,u_j)(B)\\bigr)=0\\qquad(i\\ne j).$$ For every positive sequence $\\varepsilon_n\\to0$ there are finite bordered completions $(Q_n,g_n)$, nested smooth cores $K_n\\subset G$ containing every compact subset of $G$ in their interiors eventually, normalized conformal maps $f_n:Q_n^\\circ\\to\\Omega_n$ onto finite circle domains, and smooth real tests $v_{i,n}$ on $\\Omega_n$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n v_{i,n}\\circ f_n&=u_i\n   &&\\text{near }K_n,\\label{eq:scalar-core}\\\\\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\int_{\\Omega_n\\setminus f_n(K_n)}\n             |\\nabla v_{i,n}|^2\\,\\mathrm da\n   &<\\varepsilon_n^2,\\label{eq:scalar-tail}\\\\\n \\sum_T A_{i,n}(T)^2&<\\varepsilon_n^2\n   &&(1\\le i\\le m).\\label{eq:scalar-oscillation}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $T$ ranges over the complementary round disks of $\\Omega_n$; the cluster values of $v_{i,n}$ at $T$ lie in an interval of length $A_{i,n}(T)$. The metric $g_n$ is the original metric near $K_n$. After passage to a subsequence, $f_n\\to f$ locally uniformly on $G$, where $f$ is normalized and univalent and $f(G)$ is a circle domain. All finite target holes lie in $|w|<5R$. The source-contour retention, correspondence, and bounds of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:retained-tests\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:retained-tests\">33</a><span class=\"upright\">(iv)</span> also hold. In particular, these conclusions allow arbitrarily many, and possibly uncountably many, point components in the limiting complement.",
        "source_id": "PM-071.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-scalar-retained-tests",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G\\subset\\widehat{\\mathbb C}$ contain infinity, let $X=G\\cup B$ be its quotient sphere, and fix $R$ with $\\{|z|\\ge R\\}\\cup\\{\\infty\\}\\subset G$. Let $u_1,\\ldots,u_m\\in C(X,\\mathbb R)$ be smooth on $G$, with $\\int_G|\\mathrm du_i|^2<\\infty$, and suppose $$\\label{eq:scalar-pair-null}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{area}}\\bigl((u_i,u_j)(B)\\bigr)=0\\qquad(i\\ne j).$$ For every positive sequence $\\varepsilon_n\\to0$ there are finite bordered completions $(Q_n,g_n)$, nested smooth cores $K_n\\subset G$ containing every compact subset of $G$ in their interiors eventually, normalized conformal maps $f_n:Q_n^\\circ\\to\\Omega_n$ onto finite circle domains, and smooth real tests $v_{i,n}$ on $\\Omega_n$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n v_{i,n}\\circ f_n&=u_i\n   &&\\text{near }K_n,\\label{eq:scalar-core}\\\\\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\int_{\\Omega_n\\setminus f_n(K_n)}\n             |\\nabla v_{i,n}|^2\\,\\mathrm da\n   &<\\varepsilon_n^2,\\label{eq:scalar-tail}\\\\\n \\sum_T A_{i,n}(T)^2&<\\varepsilon_n^2\n   &&(1\\le i\\le m).\\label{eq:scalar-oscillation}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $T$ ranges over the complementary round disks of $\\Omega_n$; the cluster values of $v_{i,n}$ at $T$ lie in an interval of length $A_{i,n}(T)$. The metric $g_n$ is the original metric near $K_n$. After passage to a subsequence, $f_n\\to f$ locally uniformly on $G$, where $f$ is normalized and univalent and $f(G)$ is a circle domain. All finite target holes lie in $|w|<5R$. The source-contour retention, correspondence, and bounds of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:retained-tests\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:retained-tests\">33</a><span class=\"upright\">(iv)</span> also hold. In particular, these conclusions allow arbitrarily many, and possibly uncountably many, point components in the limiting complement.",

      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $G\\subset\\widehat{\\mathbb C}$ contain infinity, let $X=G\\cup B$ be its quotient sphere, and fix $R$ with $\\{|z|\\ge R\\}\\cup\\{\\infty\\}\\subset G$. Let $u_1,\\ldots,u_m\\in C(X,\\mathbb R)$ be smooth on $G$, with $\\int_G|\\mathrm du_i|^2<\\infty$, and suppose $$\\label{eq:scalar-pair-null}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{area}}\\bigl((u_i,u_j)(B)\\bigr)=0\\qquad(i\\ne j).$$ For every positive sequence $\\varepsilon_n\\to0$ there are finite bordered completions $(Q_n,g_n)$, nested smooth cores $K_n\\subset G$ containing every compact subset of $G$ in their interiors eventually, normalized conformal maps $f_n:Q_n^\\circ\\to\\Omega_n$ onto finite circle domains, and smooth real tests $v_{i,n}$ on $\\Omega_n$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n v_{i,n}\\circ f_n&=u_i\n   &&\\text{near }K_n,\\label{eq:scalar-core}\\\\\n \\sum_{i=1}^m\\int_{\\Omega_n\\setminus f_n(K_n)}\n             |\\nabla v_{i,n}|^2\\,\\mathrm da\n   &<\\varepsilon_n^2,\\label{eq:scalar-tail}\\\\\n \\sum_T A_{i,n}(T)^2&<\\varepsilon_n^2\n   &&(1\\le i\\le m).\\label{eq:scalar-oscillation}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $T$ ranges over the complementary round disks of $\\Omega_n$; the cluster values of $v_{i,n}$ at $T$ lie in an interval of length $A_{i,n}(T)$.
